It just nags at me, and it makes it so hard to actually settle down until I finally give in and go check the box.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel you have to arrange things until they seem exactly right?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really have that issue. And the frustrating part is, I know perfectly well I already looked. My wife will be right there, waiting for me to just leave it be so we can go do something, and I still feel stuck doing it. So, yes, I definitely know it's excessive.\n\nDoctor: If you had to estimate, do these checking rituals take more than an hour a day in total?\n\nPatient: Well, like I mentioned just a moment ago, it's really hard to say for sure. It's just spread out in little chunks throughout the day, so I honestly can't give you a better estimate than what I said before.\n\nDoctor: Besides the checking itself, do you have repeated unwanted thoughts that intrude into your mind and make you anxious?\n\nPatient: Well, like I was saying earlier... yes, I do get those thoughts. I'm not really sure, to be honest. I haven't thought about it quite like that. I suppose... well, they just kind of go together. The thoughts are what make me anxious in the first place, but then feeling stuck doing the checking is incredibly frustrating. I just figured it was time I came in and talked to someone about it.\n\nDoctor: When you feel the need to check again, is it because an unwanted thought or urge suddenly pops into your mind that you might have missed something?\n\nPatient: Yes, that is pretty much exactly what happens. I mean... they're definitely coming from my own head. It's not like I'm hearing voices or someone else is putting them there, if that's what you mean. It just nags at me, and it makes it so hard to actually settle down until I finally give in and go check the box.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel you have to arrange things until they seem exactly right?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really have that issue. I mean, I like things to be tidy, but I don't feel any sort of... compulsion to arrange things until they're perfect. Well, like I was just saying a moment ago... it's awfully hard to separate them. Even if I really had to choose, I'm not sure I could give you a straight answer.

From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n17\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF42.2, F42.1, F42.0, F41.1, F60.5\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, it’s good to meet you. What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Hello. It's nice to meet you too. \n\nWell, I suppose what brought me in is that I keep finding myself checking things over and over again. It's mostly the same boxes, my day-to-day lists, and the drawers around our condo. I'll check them repeatedly before I can finally move on to whatever I'm supposed to be doing. \n\nThe frustrating part is that I know perfectly well I already looked. But I still do it, even when my wife is just standing there waiting for me to leave it be so we can leave the house. I just figured it was time I came in and talked to someone about it.\n\nDoctor: When you feel the need to check again, is it because an unwanted thought or urge suddenly pops into your mind that you might have missed something?\n\nPatient: Yes, that is pretty much exactly what happens. I keep getting these... well, these nagging thoughts. \n\nI'll suddenly worry that I might have accidentally thrown away something important from my old teaching files, or maybe that I put a needed tool in the wrong box. And I know better. I tell myself it's highly unlikely I did that, but the thought just keeps coming back anyway. It just makes it really hard to sit down and settle until I go look again.\n\nDoctor: When those nagging thoughts come up, do they feel like your own thoughts, even though they seem unreasonable?\n\nPatient: Well, yes, I suppose they do. I mean... they're definitely coming from my own head. It's not like I'm hearing voices or someone else is putting them there, if that's what you mean. It's just my own mind, second-guessing myself, even when I know better.\n\nDoctor: When these thoughts come up, do you try to resist them?\n\nPatient: I do try, yes. Like I mentioned a moment ago with those worries about my old teaching files or the tools... I try to tell myself it's unlikely and just ignore it. I'll try to just sit there and read or watch television, or whatever it is I'm doing. \n\nBut... well, no matter how much I try to push it away, the thought just keeps coming back anyway. It just nags at me, and it makes it so hard to actually settle down until I finally give in and go check the box.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel you have to arrange things until they seem exactly right?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really have that issue. I mean, I like things to be tidy, but I don't feel any sort of... compulsion to arrange things until they're perfect. It's really just the checking that bothers me.\n\nDoctor: On a typical day, about how much total time do you spend checking these boxes, lists, or drawers?\n\nPatient: Well, it adds up, I suppose. I don't know the exact minutes, but it's enough that... well, unpacking the condo has taken much longer than it really should have. And I've been putting off preparing for my volunteer tutoring at the library, and even my hobbies. I just feel like I can't move on to those things until I've verified everything enough times.\n\nDoctor: Do these checking habits feel upsetting and unwanted to you?\n\nPatient: Yes, they do. I mean, I really don't want to be doing this. It's frustrating. I just want to unpack and enjoy my retirement, but these thoughts keep getting in the way. It's definitely upsetting to feel like I can't just let it go.\n\nDoctor: Do these checking rituals take more than an hour of your day in total?\n\nPatient: I'm not entirely sure, to be honest. I haven't really looked at a clock while I'm doing it. It might add up to an hour if you put all those little moments together, or... well, maybe it's a bit less. I just know it's enough to get in the way of my day, but I really couldn't give you a precise amount of time.\n\nDoctor: Do those unwanted thoughts ever come up even when you are not checking or about to check something?\n\nPatient: Yes, they do. Um, I could just be sitting down, not doing anything, and I keep getting these nagging thoughts. I'll start worrying that I might have thrown away something important from my old teaching files, or... or maybe I put a needed tool in the wrong box while I was unpacking. And even when I tell myself it's really unlikely that I did that, the thought just comes back. It makes it very hard to just settle and relax.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that these repeated thoughts and checking are excessive or unreasonable?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do. Honestly, that's what brought me in to see you. I know it's unreasonable because... well, I find myself checking the same boxes, my lists, the drawers here in the condo over and over before I can just move on to the next thing. And the frustrating part is, I know perfectly well I already looked. My wife will be right there, waiting for me to just leave it be so we can go do something, and I still feel stuck doing it. So, yes, I definitely know it's excessive.\n\nDoctor: If you had to estimate, do these checking rituals take more than an hour a day in total?\n\nPatient: Well, like I mentioned just a moment ago, it's really hard to say for sure. If I absolutely had to guess, I suppose it could be right around an hour altogether. But I really haven't timed myself. It's just spread out in little chunks throughout the day, so I honestly can't give you a better estimate than what I said before.\n\nDoctor: Besides the checking itself, do you have repeated unwanted thoughts that intrude into your mind and make you anxious?\n\nPatient: Well, like I was saying earlier... yes, I do get those thoughts. They just pop into my head out of nowhere, even when I'm just sitting around. I start worrying about throwing away old files or misplacing tools, and it definitely makes me feel anxious and on edge. I can't seem to just shake them off, no matter how much I try to tell myself it's fine.\n\nDoctor: Which bothers you more: the unwanted thoughts themselves, or the checking you do to relieve them?\n\nPatient: Hmm... I'm not really sure, to be honest. I haven't thought about it quite like that. I suppose... well, they just kind of go together. The thoughts are what make me anxious in the first place, but then feeling stuck doing the checking is incredibly frustrating. So, I don't know if I can really pick one that bothers me more than the other.\n\nDoctor: Do these checking habits feel like they fit with the kind of person you are, or do they feel alien and not like you?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure how to answer that. I haven't ever stopped to think about it in those terms. I mean... I've always been a fairly careful person, I suppose. But \"alien\" is a strong word. It's just... well, it's just something I find myself doing now, even when I'd rather be doing something else. So I really couldn't say if it feels like me or not.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when these unwanted thoughts kept bothering you but you did not do any checking in response?\n\nPatient: No, I... I don't believe so. If the thought starts bothering me, I pretty much always end up checking. I can't really recall a time where I just sat there and let it bother me without doing anything about it. It's just... well, I just go check so I can put it out of my mind.\n\nDoctor: If you had to choose, which is more distressing for you: the intrusive thoughts, or the checking rituals?\n\nPatient: Well, like I was just saying a moment ago... it's awfully hard to separate them. Even if I really had to choose, I'm not sure I could give you a straight answer. \n\nThe thoughts start the worry, and the checking is the chore I have to do to fix it. They just go hand in hand for me. So neither one really stands out as being worse than the other. I just don't think I can pick one.\n\nDoctor: When you cannot check right away, do the thoughts themselves feel very distressing?\n\nPatient: Yes, they do. I keep getting these... these nagging thoughts that I might have thrown away something important from my old teaching files, or maybe that I put a needed tool in the wrong box. \n\nIf I can't go look right then, it just eats at me. Even when I sit there and tell myself it's highly unlikely I did anything of the sort, the thought just comes right back. It makes it very hard to settle down and focus on anything else until I can get up and check. So, yes, the thoughts themselves are quite bothersome.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

I'll try to just sit there and read or watch television, or whatever it is I'm doing. \n\nBut... well, no matter how much I try to push it away, the thought just keeps coming back anyway. It just nags at me, and it makes it so hard to actually settle down until I finally give in and go check the box.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel you have to arrange things until they seem exactly right?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really have that issue. I mean, I like things to be tidy, but I don't feel any sort of... compulsion to arrange things until they're perfect. It's really just the checking that bothers me.\n\nDoctor: On a typical day, about how much total time do you spend checking these boxes, lists, or drawers?\n\nPatient: Well, it adds up, I suppose. I don't know the exact minutes, but it's enough that... well, unpacking the condo has taken much longer than it really should have. And I've been putting off preparing for my volunteer tutoring at the library, and even my hobbies. I just feel like I can't move on to those things until I've verified everything enough times.\n\nDoctor: Do these checking habits feel upsetting and unwanted to you?\n\nPatient: Yes, they do. I mean, I really don't want to be doing this. It's frustrating. I just want to unpack and enjoy my retirement, but these thoughts keep getting in the way. It's definitely upsetting to feel like I can't just let it go.\n\nDoctor: Do these checking rituals take more than an hour of your day in total?\n\nPatient: I'm not entirely sure, to be honest. I haven't really looked at a clock while I'm doing it. It might add up to an hour if you put all those little moments together, or... well, maybe it's a bit less. I just know it's enough to get in the way of my day, but I really couldn't give you a precise amount of time.\n\nDoctor: Do those unwanted thoughts ever come up even when you are not checking or about to check something?\n\nPatient: Yes, they do. Um, I could just be sitting down, not doing anything, and I keep getting these nagging thoughts. I'll start worrying that I might have thrown away something important from my old teaching files, or... or maybe I put a needed tool in the wrong box while I was unpacking. And even when I tell myself it's really unlikely that I did that, the thought just comes back. It makes it very hard to just settle and relax.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that these repeated thoughts and checking are excessive or unreasonable?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do. Honestly, that's what brought me in to see you. I know it's unreasonable because... well, I find myself checking the same boxes, my lists, the drawers here in the condo over and over before I can just move on to the next thing. And the frustrating part is, I know perfectly well I already looked. My wife will be right there, waiting for me to just leave it be so we can go do something, and I still feel stuck doing it. So, yes, I definitely know it's excessive.\n\nDoctor: If you had to estimate, do these checking rituals take more than an hour a day in total?\n\nPatient: Well, like I mentioned just a moment ago, it's really hard to say for sure. If I absolutely had to guess, I suppose it could be right around an hour altogether. But I really haven't timed myself. It's just spread out in little chunks throughout the day, so I honestly can't give you a better estimate than what I said before.\n\nDoctor: Besides the checking itself, do you have repeated unwanted thoughts that intrude into your mind and make you anxious?\n\nPatient: Well, like I was saying earlier... yes, I do get those thoughts. They just pop into my head out of nowhere, even when I'm just sitting around. I start worrying about throwing away old files or misplacing tools, and it definitely makes me feel anxious and on edge. I can't seem to just shake them off, no matter how much I try to tell myself it's fine.\n\nDoctor: Which bothers you more: the unwanted thoughts themselves, or the checking you do to relieve them?\n\nPatient: Hmm... The thoughts are what make me anxious in the first place, but then feeling stuck doing the checking is incredibly frustrating. So, I don't know if I can really pick one that bothers me more than the other.\n\nDoctor: Do these checking habits feel like they fit with the kind of person you are, or do they feel alien and not like you?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure how to answer that. I haven't ever stopped to think about it in those terms. I mean... I've always been a fairly careful person, I suppose. But \"alien\" is a strong word. It's just... well, it's just something I find myself doing now, even when I'd rather be doing something else. So I really couldn't say if it feels like me or not.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when these unwanted thoughts kept bothering you but you did not do any checking in response?\n\nPatient: No, I... I don't believe so. If the thought starts bothering me, I pretty much always end up checking. I can't really recall a time where I just sat there and let it bother me without doing anything about it. It's just... well, I just go check so I can put it out of my mind.\n\nDoctor: If you had to choose, which is more distressing for you: the intrusive thoughts, or the checking rituals?\n\nPatient: Well, like I was just saying a moment ago... it's awfully hard to separate them. Even if I really had to choose, I'm not sure I could give you a straight answer. \n\nThe thoughts start the worry, and the checking is the chore I have to do to fix it. They just go hand in hand for me. So neither one really stands out as being worse than the other. I just don't think I can pick one.\n\nDoctor: When you cannot check right away, do the thoughts themselves feel very distressing?\n\nPatient: Yes, they do. I'm retired now, and I'd much rather spend my day relaxing or doing things I enjoy, instead of having to get up and check on old files or tools just to get some peace of mind. I don't know the exact minutes, but it's enough that... well, unpacking the condo has taken much longer than it really should have. And I've been putting off preparing for my volunteer tutoring at the library, and even my hobbies. I just feel like I can't move on to those things until I've verified everything enough times.\n\nDoctor: Do these checking habits feel upsetting and unwanted to you?\n\nPatient: Yes, they do. I mean, I really don't want to be doing this. It's frustrating. I just want to unpack and enjoy my retirement, but these thoughts keep getting in the way. It's definitely upsetting to feel like I can't just let it go.\n\nDoctor: Do these checking rituals take more than an hour of your day in total?\n\nPatient: I'm not entirely sure, to be honest. I haven't really looked at a clock while I'm doing it. It might add up to an hour if you put all those little moments together, or... well, maybe it's a bit less. I just know it's enough to get in the way of my day, but I really couldn't give you a precise amount of time.\n\nDoctor: Do those unwanted thoughts ever come up even when you are not checking or about to check something?\n\nPatient: Yes, they do. Um, I could just be sitting down, not doing anything, and I keep getting these nagging thoughts. I'll start worrying that I might have thrown away something important from my old teaching files, or... or maybe I put a needed tool in the wrong box while I was unpacking. And even when I tell myself it's really unlikely that I did that, the thought just comes back. It makes it very hard to just settle and relax.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that these repeated thoughts and checking are excessive or unreasonable?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do. Honestly, that's what brought me in to see you. I know it's unreasonable because... well, I find myself checking the same boxes, my lists, the drawers here in the condo over and over before I can just move on to the next thing. And the frustrating part is, I know perfectly well I already looked. My wife will be right there, waiting for me to just leave it be so we can go do something, and I still feel stuck doing it. So, yes, I definitely know it's excessive.\n\nDoctor: If you had to estimate, do these checking rituals take more than an hour a day in total?\n\nPatient: Well, like I mentioned just a moment ago, it's really hard to say for sure. If I absolutely had to guess, I suppose it could be right around an hour altogether. But I really haven't timed myself. It's just spread out in little chunks throughout the day, so I honestly can't give you a better estimate than what I said before.\n\nDoctor: Besides the checking itself, do you have repeated unwanted thoughts that intrude into your mind and make you anxious?\n\nPatient: Well, like I was saying earlier... yes, I do get those thoughts. They just pop into my head out of nowhere, even when I'm just sitting around. I start worrying about throwing away old files or misplacing tools, and it definitely makes me feel anxious and on edge. I can't seem to just shake them off, no matter how much I try to tell myself it's fine.\n\nDoctor: Which bothers you more: the unwanted thoughts themselves, or the checking you do to relieve them?\n\nPatient: Hmm... I'm not really sure, to be honest. I haven't thought about it quite like that. I suppose... well, they just kind of go together. The thoughts are what make me anxious in the first place, but then feeling stuck doing the checking is incredibly frustrating. So, I don't know if I can really pick one that bothers me more than the other.\n\nDoctor: Do these checking habits feel like they fit with the kind of person you are, or do they feel alien and not like you?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure how to answer that. I haven't ever stopped to think about it in those terms. I mean... I've always been a fairly careful person, I suppose. But \"alien\" is a strong word. It's just... well, it's just something I find myself doing now, even when I'd rather be doing something else. So I really couldn't say if it feels like me or not.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when these unwanted thoughts kept bothering you but you did not do any checking in response?\n\nPatient: No, I... I don't believe so. If the thought starts bothering me, I pretty much always end up checking. I can't really recall a time where I just sat there and let it bother me without doing anything about it. It's just... well, I just go check so I can put it out of my mind.\n\nDoctor: If you had to choose, which is more distressing for you: the intrusive thoughts, or the checking rituals?\n\nPatient: Well, like I was just saying a moment ago... it's awfully hard to separate them. Even if I really had to choose, I'm not sure I could give you a straight answer. \n\nThe thoughts start the worry, and the checking is the chore I have to do to fix it. They just go hand in hand for me. So neither one really stands out as being worse than the other. I just don't think I can pick one.\n\nDoctor: When you cannot check right away, do the thoughts themselves feel very distressing?\n\nPatient: Yes, they do. I keep getting these... these nagging thoughts that I might have thrown away something important from my old teaching files, or maybe that I put a needed tool in the wrong box. \n\nIf I can't go look right then, it just eats at me. Even when I sit there and tell myself it's highly unlikely I did anything of the sort, the thought just comes right back. It makes it very hard to settle down and focus on anything else until I can get up and check. So, yes, the thoughts themselves are quite bothersome.\n\nDoctor: Do you wish these thoughts and checking would stop?\n\nPatient: Yes, I certainly do. It's... well, it's just a nuisance, really. I mean... they're definitely coming from my own head. It's not like I'm hearing voices or someone else is putting them there, if that's what you mean. It's just my own mind, second-guessing myself, even when I know better.\n\nDoctor: When these thoughts come up, do you try to resist them?\n\nPatient: I do try, yes. Like I mentioned a moment ago with those worries about my old teaching files or the tools... I try to tell myself it's unlikely and just ignore it. I'll try to just sit there and read or watch television, or whatever it is I'm doing. \n\nBut... well, no matter how much I try to push it away, the thought just keeps coming back anyway. It just nags at me, and it makes it so hard to actually settle down until I finally give in and go check the box.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel you have to arrange things until they seem exactly right?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really have that issue. I mean, I like things to be tidy, but I don't feel any sort of... compulsion to arrange things until they're perfect. It's really just the checking that bothers me.\n\nDoctor: On a typical day, about how much total time do you spend checking these boxes, lists, or drawers?\n\nPatient: Well, it adds up, I suppose. I don't know the exact minutes, but it's enough that... well, unpacking the condo has taken much longer than it really should have. And I've been putting off preparing for my volunteer tutoring at the library, and even my hobbies. I just feel like I can't move on to those things until I've verified everything enough times.\n\nDoctor: Do these checking habits feel upsetting and unwanted to you?\n\nPatient: Yes, they do. I mean, I really don't want to be doing this. It's frustrating. I just want to unpack and enjoy my retirement, but these thoughts keep getting in the way. It's definitely upsetting to feel like I can't just let it go.\n\nDoctor: Do these checking rituals take more than an hour of your day in total?\n\nPatient: I'm not entirely sure, to be honest. I haven't really looked at a clock while I'm doing it. It might add up to an hour if you put all those little moments together, or... well, maybe it's a bit less. I just know it's enough to get in the way of my day, but I really couldn't give you a precise amount of time.\n\nDoctor: Do those unwanted thoughts ever come up even when you are not checking or about to check something?\n\nPatient: Yes, they do. Um, I could just be sitting down, not doing anything, and I keep getting these nagging thoughts. I'll start worrying that I might have thrown away something important from my old teaching files, or... or maybe I put a needed tool in the wrong box while I was unpacking. And even when I tell myself it's really unlikely that I did that, the thought just comes back. It makes it very hard to just settle and relax.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that these repeated thoughts and checking are excessive or unreasonable?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do. So, yes, I definitely know it's excessive.\n\nDoctor: If you had to estimate, do these checking rituals take more than an hour a day in total?\n\nPatient: Well, like I mentioned just a moment ago, it's really hard to say for sure. It's just spread out in little chunks throughout the day, so I honestly can't give you a better estimate than what I said before.\n\nDoctor: Besides the checking itself, do you have repeated unwanted thoughts that intrude into your mind and make you anxious?\n\nPatient: Well, like I was saying earlier... yes, I do get those thoughts. It's just... well, it's just something I find myself doing now, even when I'd rather be doing something else. So I really couldn't say if it feels like me or not.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when these unwanted thoughts kept bothering you but you did not do any checking in response?\n\nPatient: No, I... I don't believe so. If the thought starts bothering me, I pretty much always end up checking. I can't really recall a time where I just sat there and let it bother me without doing anything about it. It's just... well, I just go check so I can put it out of my mind.\n\nDoctor: If you had to choose, which is more distressing for you: the intrusive thoughts, or the checking rituals?\n\nPatient: Well, like I was just saying a moment ago... it's awfully hard to separate them.
